# Paul Van Meerbergen

Councillor, Ward 10 (2022–2026 boundaries) · [Full voting record on Open Council →](/councillors/current/p-van-meerbergen)

> **This is a descriptive record, not an endorsement.** Every pattern below is built from real recorded votes since 2023, translated from raw yea/nay into what the vote actually did (see [What Council Actually Controls](/election/what-council-controls) for how much of this any of them controls). It says nothing about a councillor's reasons, character, or fitness for office — only how they voted. Votes with no clear direction ("unclear") are excluded from the pattern counts but listed in their own section below for transparency. Every row links to its source: to the heading for the specific agenda item the motion belongs to (several motion parts under one item normally share that one heading — that's how the source pages are laid out, not an error), or, where an item number is reused for two genuinely different, unrelated motions with no way to tell them apart, to the meeting page as a whole instead (the row says so when that happens).

### [Housing & Density](/election/issues/housing)

*Of the 136 divided votes on this issue since 2023 that had a clear direction, this councillor has a recorded position on 74 of them (4 of these are excluded from the pattern counts — see below). 1 recused, 12 absent. The other 49: 4 were motions at a meeting where this councillor was listed as also present (no vote recorded); 45 were motions with no recorded vote for this councillor at that meeting.*

#### approved the development, rezoning, or housing-policy item vs. denied the development, rezoning, or housing-policy item

Of 34 divided votes since 2023 where the motion's effect on this axis was clear, this councillor supported 26 measures that would have approved the development, rezoning, or housing-policy item; opposed 7 measures that would have approved the development, rezoning, or housing-policy item; opposed 1 measure that would have denied the development, rezoning, or housing-policy item (absent 7).

#### increased permitted density or building height vs. reduced permitted density or building height

Of 28 divided votes since 2023 where the motion's effect on this axis was clear, this councillor supported 22 measures that would have increased permitted density or building height; opposed 4 measures that would have increased permitted density or building height; supported 1 measure that would have reduced permitted density or building height; opposed 1 measure that would have reduced permitted density or building height (recused 1 (recused from discussing or voting on this item; the reason isn't recorded in this data), absent 1).

### [Taxes & Budget](/election/issues/budget)

*Of the 86 divided votes on this issue since 2023 that had a clear direction, this councillor has a recorded position on 83 of them (15 of these are excluded from the pattern counts — see below). 0 recused, 3 absent.*

> Budget votes since 2024 are votes on **amendments** to the Mayor's tabled budget under Ontario's strong-mayor powers, not on an independently council-drafted budget — see [what that changes about what a budget vote means](/election/what-council-controls#who-tables-the-budget).

#### increased the tax levy or a budget item vs. reduced the tax levy or a budget item

Of 63 divided votes since 2023 where the motion's effect on this axis was clear, this councillor supported 4 measures that would have increased the tax levy or a budget item; opposed 32 measures that would have increased the tax levy or a budget item; supported 22 measures that would have reduced the tax levy or a budget item; opposed 5 measures that would have reduced the tax levy or a budget item.

**15 votes excluded from the pattern above:** within a same-day, same-subject group of motions (an amendment offering different wordings of the same decision), this councillor's votes pointed in more than one direction on this axis — neither a "for" nor an "against" that the pattern sentence can honestly claim, so none of them are counted in the table or sentence above. ### [Homelessness & Encampments](/election/issues/encampments)

*Of the 59 divided votes on this issue since 2023 that had a clear direction, this councillor has a recorded position on 45 of them (16 of these are excluded from the pattern counts — see below). 0 recused, 8 absent. The other 6: 6 were motions with no recorded vote for this councillor at that meeting.*

#### expanded the encampment/homelessness response vs. reduced the encampment/homelessness response

Of 21 divided votes since 2023 where the motion's effect on this axis was clear, this councillor supported 8 measures that would have expanded the encampment/homelessness response; opposed 9 measures that would have expanded the encampment/homelessness response; supported 4 measures that would have reduced the encampment/homelessness response (absent 2).

**16 votes excluded from the pattern above:** within a same-day, same-subject group of motions (an amendment offering different wordings of the same decision), this councillor's votes pointed in more than one direction on this axis — neither a "for" nor an "against" that the pattern sentence can honestly claim, so none of them are counted in the table or sentence above. Shown here instead of hidden:

### [Transit & Roads](/election/issues/transit)

*Of the 41 divided votes on this issue since 2023 that had a clear direction, this councillor has a recorded position on 39 of them (9 of these are excluded from the pattern counts — see below). 0 recused, 2 absent.*

#### expanded transit or road service/infrastructure vs. reduced transit or road service/infrastructure

Of 17 divided votes since 2023 where the motion's effect on this axis was clear, this councillor supported 5 measures that would have expanded transit or road service/infrastructure; opposed 9 measures that would have expanded transit or road service/infrastructure; supported 1 measure that would have reduced transit or road service/infrastructure; opposed 2 measures that would have reduced transit or road service/infrastructure.

**9 votes excluded from the pattern above:** within a same-day, same-subject group of motions (an amendment offering different wordings of the same decision), this councillor's votes pointed in more than one direction on this axis — neither a "for" nor an "against" that the pattern sentence can honestly claim, so none of them are counted in the table or sentence above. #### approved the transit/roads item vs. denied the transit/roads item

Of 13 divided votes since 2023 where the motion's effect on this axis was clear, this councillor supported 1 measure that would have approved the transit/roads item; opposed 12 measures that would have approved the transit/roads item (absent 2). No motion on this axis since 2023 would have denied the transit/roads item — every direction-bearing motion here would have approved the transit/roads item.

---

*Sample sizes above count only votes where Paul Van Meerbergen cast a yea or nay on a motion the verified per-motion classification (see [methodology](/election/issues)) could place on a clear "for"/"against" axis. A recusal means the councillor formally withdrew from discussing and voting on that item — this data does not record why, so no reason is asserted here — and, like an absence, is shown separately in each issue's summary line and never counted as a position. Only sitting councillors get a stance profile on this hub; challengers don't yet have a council voting record to summarize.*

---

Methodology: A "divided" vote is any council or committee motion since January 1, 2023 that wasn't approved unanimously and wasn't a routine procedural step, and it doesn't include a vote decided by secret ballot to fill an appointment, since those don't produce a recorded yea or nay by councillor. For each councillor, on each issue, on each axis: a vote counts as "for" when it matched that axis's more permissive outcome, and "against" when it matched the more restrictive one — but a nay is never described as if it enacted the restrictive outcome; it's described as opposing the motion it was cast on, and the same care is taken in the other direction. When every recorded vote on an axis since 2023 happens to fall on one side, the pattern sentence says so plainly instead of silently reporting a zero on the other side. Every motion's issue, axis, and direction ("what a yea did") come from an independent, motion-by-motion review, checked against that motion's own complete text in the official meeting record rather than a keyword search, with a small, separately published list of after-the-fact corrections applied on top — never a silent edit to the reviewed record. This is a genuine reading of what each clause did, not a raw yea/nay count, including for the generic "approved or denied the item" description used when no issue-specific axis applies. Recusals and absences are always counted on their own, never folded into "against" and never treated as a hidden position. A pattern sentence is only stated once a councillor has at least 5 separate underlying decisions on an axis — a committee vote followed later by a council vote on the same policy question counts as one decision, not two, so a motion with several stages can't inflate the count; below that threshold the individual votes are still shown, just without a summary sentence. Some motions were reviewed and confirmed to have no clear direction at all — a referral, a request for more information, or a genuinely ambiguous clause — and these are listed, on every relevant issue, as motions with no clear direction: never counted in any pattern or sample size, but never hidden either. This project has no source recording which committee a councillor belongs to — that list isn't published or collected anywhere; a meeting's own attendance record shows who was there that day, not who belongs to it. So when a councillor has no recorded vote on a motion, nothing is claimed about why, in either direction: only whether that meeting's own record separately listed this councillor as present without a recorded vote, or whether nothing else can be said at all. Before any of the above, motions were also dropped in three ways, none of them guessed at or repaired: 33 where the same person was recorded as voting more than one way on the same motion (a roster conflict in the source data); 0 more where a motion's own written result didn't match its recorded yea/nay count (a result mismatch); and 3 more that turned out, on review, not to be a genuine division at all — a lopsided result the source data's own "unanimous" marker missed, or the same motion recorded twice under two item numbers. Each motion is filed under exactly one issue, so a motion that touches two topics — a housing decision with a budget line, say — appears on one issue page only, never both. Where the source text has a hash symbol immediately followed by a case or item number — as in a title reading "Business Case", then a hash, then "P-5" — this hub shows it instead as "Business Case No. P-5", so the character can't be misread as a link; the underlying wording is not otherwise changed.
